It has not disappeared from the nonlinear equation\nthat generates \\(a^{(2)}\\).\n\n## 2. What time regularity proves\n\nOn \\(0\\le s\\le S\\), impose the primal bounds\n\\(\\|W^{(2)}(s)\\|_{\\rm op},\\|W^{(3)}(s)\\|_{\\rm op},\n\\|W^{(4)}(s)\\|_\\infty\\le B_S\\), with \\(B_S\\) independent of\n\\(n\\). These are the established actual-trajectory bounds on the\ncorresponding primal event. All four paths in (7) then have derivative\nEuclidean norm divided by \\(\\sqrt n\\) bounded by a constant \\(C_S\\)\nindependent of \\(n\\). For the new path, boundedness of \\(\\phi'\\)\ngives \\(\\|\\delta^{(2)}\\|_2/\\sqrt n\\le B_S^2\\), so, for\n\\(0\\le t\\le s\\le S\\),\n\\[\n\\frac{\\|a^{(2)}(s)-a^{(2)}(t)\\|_2}{\\sqrt n}\n\\le\\int_t^s\\frac{\\|\\delta^{(2)}(u)\\|_2}{\\sqrt n}\\,du\n\\le C_S|s-t|.\n\\tag{8}\n\\]\nThe other three derivative bounds were established in\nPANAHI_EULER_PERTURBATION_SIZE.md. Constants are uniform in width on\nthe stated primal event. These are statements about actual unperturbed\ntrajectories; they are not assumed for a perturbed comparison process.\n\nLet \\(\\pi(s)\\) be the last mesh point at or before \\(s\\), for a\ndeterministic mesh of spacing at most \\(\\varepsilon/C_S\\). Each of\nthe four query paths changes by Euclidean norm at most\n\\(\\sqrt n\\varepsilon\\) between \\(\\pi(s)\\) and \\(s\\). In particular,\n\\[\n\\begin{split}\n\\sup_{s\\le S}\n \\frac{\\|W^{(2)}_0[h^{(1)}(s)-h^{(1)}(\\pi(s))]\\|_2}{\\sqrt n}\n &\\le\\|W^{(2)}_0\\|_{\\rm op}\\varepsilon,\\\\\n\\sup_{s\\le S}\n \\frac{\\|(W^{(2)}_0)^T[a^{(2)}(s)-a^{(2)}(\\pi(s))]\\|_2}{\\sqrt n}\n &\\le\\|W^{(2)}_0\\|_{\\rm op}\\varepsilon.\n\\end{split}                                                    \\tag{9}\n\\]\nThe two corresponding bounds for \\(W^{(3)}_0h^{(2)}\\) and\n\\((W^{(3)}_0)^T\\delta^{(3)}\\) have right-hand side\n\\(\\|W^{(3)}_0\\|_{\\rm op}\\varepsilon\\).\nConsequently a specified actual path admits a time net of\n\\(1+\\lceil C_SS/\\varepsilon\\rceil\\) matrix arguments per\norientation, independently of width. This is an approximation of\nmatrix responses along a supplied trajectory.\n\nThe rank memories in (1)--(2) do not hide an extra continuity failure\non this uniformly Lipschitz path class. For example, compare two\npaths with their corresponding tilded memories. Suppose both paths\nobey\n\\[\n\\frac{\\|h^{(1)}(s)\\|_2}{\\sqrt n}\\le B_h,\\qquad\n\\frac{\\|(h^{(1)})'(s)\\|_2}{\\sqrt n}\\le L_h,\\qquad\n\\frac{\\|(a^{(2)})'(s)\\|_2}{\\sqrt n}\\le L_a,\n\\]\nand the same bounds hold for the tilded quantities. Define the two\nscalar errors\n\\[\ne_a=\\sup_{s\\le S}\n \\frac{\\|a^{(2)}(s)-\\widetilde a^{(2)}(s)\\|_2}{\\sqrt n},\\qquad\ne_h=\\sup_{s\\le S}\n \\frac{\\|h^{(1)}(s)-\\widetilde h^{(1)}(s)\\|_2}{\\sqrt n}.\n\\]\nWith both primitives zero initially, integration by parts gives\n\\[\n\\begin{split}\nM^{(2)}(s)-\\widetilde M^{(2)}(s)\n={}&\\frac{[a^{(2)}(s)-\\widetilde a^{(2)}(s)](h^{(1)}(s))^T}{n}\\\\\n &-\\int_0^s\n  \\frac{[a^{(2)}-\\widetilde a^{(2)}]((h^{(1)})')^T}{n}\\,du\\\\\n &+\\int_0^s\n  \\frac{(\\widetilde a^{(2)})'[h^{(1)}-\\widetilde h^{(1)}]^T}{n}\\,du,\n\\end{split}\n\\]\nwhere all integrand factors are evaluated at \\(u\\). Hence\n\\[\n\\sup_s\\|M^{(2)}-\\widetilde M^{(2)}\\|_{\\rm op}\n\\le(B_h+SL_h)e_a+SL_ae_h.                                   \\tag{10}\n\\]\nSimilarly, since\n\\(R^{(1)}(s)=\\int_0^s(M^{(2)}(u))^T(a^{(2)})'(u)du\\),\n\\[\n\\sup_{s\\le S}\\frac{\\|R^{(1)}(s)-\\widetilde R^{(1)}(s)\\|_2}{\\sqrt n}\n\\le SL_a\\sup_s\\|M^{(2)}-\\widetilde M^{(2)}\\|_{\n\\rm op}+2SL_aB_h e_a.                                      \\tag{11}\n\\]\nFor (11), integrate\n\\(\\int_0^s(\\widetilde M^{(2)})^T\n [(a^{(2)})'-(\\widetilde a^{(2)})']du\\) by parts, using\n\\(\\|\\widetilde M^{(2)}\\|_{\\rm op}\\le SL_aB_h\\) and\n\\(\\|(\\widetilde M^{(2)})'\\|_{\\rm op}\\le L_aB_h\\).\nFor completeness, if both top-layer paths satisfy\n\\(\\|h^{(2)}\\|_2/\\sqrt n\\le B_2\\) and\n\\(\\|\\delta^{(3)}\\|_2/\\sqrt n\\le D_3\\), then the ordinary\nintegral for \\(M^{(3)}\\) gives\n\\[\n\\begin{split}\n\\sup_{s\\le S}\\|M^{(3)}(s)-\\widetilde M^{(3)}(s)\\|_{\\rm op}\n\\le{}&SB_2\\sup_{s\\le S}\n \\frac{\\|\\delta^{(3)}(s)-\\widetilde\\delta^{(3)}(s)\\|_2}{\\sqrt n}\\\\\n &+SD_3\\sup_{s\\le S}\n \\frac{\\|h^{(2)}(s)-\\widetilde h^{(2)}(s)\\|_2}{\\sqrt n}.\n\\end{split}\n\\]\n\nThus (9) yields small equation residuals after replacing only the\ninitial-matrix responses along the actual trajectory. The exact\nrank-update memory has not been dropped to obtain this statement.\n\n## 3. Why this does not yet construct a finite-query approximation\n\nAt time \\(s_k\\), the value of an actual query such as \\(a^{(2)}(s_k)\\)\nhas been generated by the dynamics on the entire preceding interval.\nThose dynamics used the initial matrices at unrecorded intermediate\narguments. Observing the actual query is causal in physical time,\nbut it is not thereby measurable from only the retained finite-query\ntranscript. Gaussian conditioning on the latter transcript cannot\ntreat that query as a legal next query without proving this\nmeasurability. Sampling a true trajectory does not supply such a proof.\n\nA finite-call algorithm can instead evolve its own state using frozen\nor interpolated matrix responses and make new calls on its own\nqueries. That is an admissible proposed approximation. Equation (9)\non the true path proves consistency of the substitution, not closeness\nbetween this algorithm and the true dynamics. In particular it does\nnot establish the rank premise for the actual perturbed histories in\nPANAHI_ADAPTIVE_GRAM_MARTINGALE.md; query perturbations may generate\nnew directions in subsequent nonlinear queries.\n\nThe uniform temporal bounds also do not by themselves give strong\ncompactness in a fixed infinite-dimensional Hilbert space. For example,\nin \\(\\ell^2(\\mathbb N)\\), the paths \\(s\\mapsto s e_j\\), where\n\\(e_j\\) is the \\(j\\)-th standard unit vector, are uniformly bounded\nand Lipschitz on \\([0,S]\\), but their values at any positive time\nhave no norm-convergent subsequence. This observation is not a claim\nthat such a family occurs in the canonical network, nor a construction\nof a population space for it.\n\n## 4. The retained stability term and derivative observables\n\nFor two candidate state paths, the exact primitive difference obeys\n\\[\n\\begin{split}\na^{(2)}(s)-\\widetilde a^{(2)}(s)=\\int_0^s\\bigl\\{\n &\\phi'(z^{(2)})\\odot(q^{(2)}-\\widetilde q^{(2)})\\\\\n &+[\\phi'(z^{(2)})-\\phi'(\\widetilde z^{(2)})]\n       \\odot\\widetilde q^{(2)}\\bigr\\}\\,du.\n\\end{split}                                                    \\tag{12}\n\\]\nAll factors inside the integral are evaluated at \\(u\\).\nThe first integrand has Euclidean norm divided by \\(\\sqrt n\\)\nat most \\(\\|q^{(2)}-\\widetilde q^{(2)}\\|_2/\\sqrt n\\).\nIn the second integrand, the available bound on\n\\(\\|\\widetilde q^{(2)}\\|_2/\\sqrt n\\) does not provide a\nwidth-uniform Lipschitz bound in\n\\(\\|z^{(2)}-\\widetilde z^{(2)}\\|_2/\\sqrt n\\).\nFor \\(Q>0\\) the exact tail split gives\n\\[\n\\begin{split}\n&\\frac{\\|[\\phi'(z^{(2)})-\\phi'(\\widetilde z^{(2)})]\n       \\odot\\widetilde q^{(2)}\\|_2}{\\sqrt n}\\\\\n&\\quad\\le\\|\\phi''\\|_\\infty Q\n  \\frac{\\|z^{(2)}-\\widetilde z^{(2)}\\|_2}{\\sqrt n}\n +2\\frac{\\|\\widetilde q^{(2)}\\odot\n   \\mathbf1_{\\{|\\widetilde q^{(2)}|>Q\\}}\\|_2}{\\sqrt n}.\n\\end{split}                                                    \\tag{13}\n\\]\nThe total L2 bound alone neither makes the last term uniformly small\nover the approximation family nor supplies the tail-versus-stability\nrate needed to pass \\(Q\\to\\infty\\). Integrating the initial query\ntherefore retains the actual middle multiplier; it does not resolve\nthe continuation problem represented by that multiplier.\n\nThere is also a separate observable issue. The lower backward result\nis\n\\[\n(W^{(2)}(s))^T\\delta^{(2)}(s)\n=\\frac{d}{ds}\\big[(W^{(2)}_0)^T a^{(2)}(s)\\big]\n +(M^{(2)}(s))^T(a^{(2)})'(s),                             \\tag{14}\n\\]\nand \\(\\delta^{(1)}=\\phi'(z^{(1)})\\odot\n(W^{(2)})^T\\delta^{(2)}\\). Uniform approximation of the integrated\nresponse in (9) does not imply strong convergence of its derivative.\nFor example, \\(j^{-1}\\sin(js)v\\) tends uniformly to zero with\nuniformly bounded derivative, while its derivatives do not tend to\nzero in time L2 for a nonzero fixed \\(v\\). This is a limitation of\nthe inference, not a canonical counterexample. Hidden velocities and\nthe raw first kernel block require additional derivative or energy\ncontrol even if uniform state convergence were separately proved.\n\n## Conclusion\n\nThe exact initial-matrix state queries can all be made time-Lipschitz\nby passing to the lower backward primitive. Their supplied-trajectory\ncovering number is width-independent, and the full rank memories have\nthe continuity bounds (10)--(11). This improves the representation.\nIt does not establish a finite-transcript causal approximation,\nnonlinear removal, strong compactness, unique autonomous restart, or\nthe required velocity/kernel convergence. The decisive retained term\nis (12), with the distinct derivative obligation (14).\n", "truncated": false}}

Do not use for teaching a technical source, solving an isolated mathematical problem, reviewing an external paper, or summarizing material that needs no research-state reconciliation.\n---\n\n# Investigate Conjectures\n\n## Purpose\n\nTurn a broad research question into a precise, falsifiable conjecture and an efficient program for resolving its weakest links. Integrate theory, adversarial reasoning, and only authorized computation. Preserve uncertainty: an honest open or inconclusive result is preferable to a stronger unsupported claim.\n\nTreat the skill as the research-program controller. Use a specialized proof, teaching, paper-review, data, or coding skill for bounded subtasks when appropriate, while retaining the claim structure and evidence updates here.\n\n## Load references selectively\n\n- Read [research-contract.md](references/research-contract.md) when starting, materially reformulating, or checking the non-vacuity of a conjecture.\n- Read [evidence-ledger.md](references/evidence-ledger.md) when resuming prior work, reconciling contradictory artifacts, updating conclusions, or writing a master report.\n- Read [adversarial-audit.md](references/adversarial-audit.md) before declaring a loophole closed, a route impossible, a result decisive, or a theorem nearly established.\n- Read [decisive-experiments.md](references/decisive-experiments.md) only when computation is explicitly authorized or when the user asks for an experimental design. Do not run an experiment merely because it would be informative.\n- Read [proof-search-orchestration.md](references/proof-search-orchestration.md) when the user requests a bounded multi-route search for a complete mathematical proof. Use parallel agents only when the user explicitly authorizes delegation or parallel work; otherwise apply the same registry and round discipline sequentially.\n\n## Establish scope and authority\n\n1. Restate the research target in one sentence.\n2. Determine whether the user authorized:\n   - read-only synthesis or assessment;\n   - new theoretical analysis;\n   - experiment design only;\n   - implementation or computation;\n   - delegated or parallel proof search;\n   - a bounded continuation under conditional branches.\n3. Record any time, compute, token, data, model, or run budget as a hard constraint.\n4. Ask only for a missing choice that would materially change the result. Otherwise make the narrowest reasonable assumption and label it.\n5. Never expand a diagnostic or reporting request into implementation, computation, external actions, or a new research branch.\n\n## Core workflow\n\n### 1. Restore the authoritative state\n\nRead the current primary artifacts before reasoning from memory. Identify the canonical statement, latest valid methods, raw evidence, unresolved objections, and superseded conclusions. Prefer derivations and raw results over summaries; prefer audited later results only when they actually repair or invalidate earlier work.\n\nWhen resuming or synthesizing, maintain the ledger in [evidence-ledger.md](references/evidence-ledger.md).\n\n### 2. Freeze the research contract\n\nSpecify the canonical object, admissible inputs, observables, error criterion, topology or norm, horizon, limit order, approximation family, coefficient provenance, complexity dependence, and forbidden substitutions. State what information the proposed surrogate may retain and whether it must be autonomous and restartable.\n\nApply the non-vacuity checks in [research-contract.md](references/research-contract.md). Reject oracle playback, hidden encodings of the target trajectory, replacement by an easier model, or quantifiers that avoid the intended compression problem.\n\n### 3. Build the claim ladder\n\nSeparate at least:\n\n1. exact identities or finite constructions;\n2. well-posedness or internal correctness of the proposed approximation;\n3. empirical accuracy on tested instances;\n4. convergence of the approximation hierarchy;\n5. identification with the intended limit or real system;\n6. compact-horizon validity;\n7. all-time or uniform validity;\n8. existence of some admissible witness versus success of the current witness.\n\nDo not promote evidence from one rung to another without an explicit bridge.\n\n### 4. State the sharp conjecture\n\nWrite explicit quantifiers and dependencies. State:\n\n- the admissible problem class;\n- what may depend on accuracy, horizon, and regularity;\n- what must remain independent of ambient scale or inaccessible trajectory information;\n- the approximation guarantee and norm;\n- coefficient provenance and initialization;\n- autonomy, restartability, or finite-memory requirements;\n- concrete falsifiers.\n\nIf several statements are needed, identify one central conjecture and label the rest as lemmas, assumptions, or stronger extensions.\n\n### 5. Derive the exact causal skeleton\n\nDerive before truncating. Mark each step as exact, conditional on named assumptions, heuristic, empirical, or open. Track causal dependencies, information flow, history dependence, and the source of every residual.\n\nKeep distinct approximation axes distinct. Convergence in time step, basis degree, perturbation order, response depth, width, spatial resolution, or another coordinate does not imply convergence in any other axis.\n\nIn particular, separate:\n\n- correctness of every fixed-order coefficient from convergence of the full expansion;\n- production of omitted error from propagation or stability of that error;\n- observable accuracy from state-space convergence;\n- failure of a proof route from falsity of the conjecture.\n\n### 6. Build a typed evidence ledger\n\nFor each central claim, record its exact statement, claim-ladder rung, status, assumptions, supporting and contrary evidence, dependencies, cheapest resolver, falsifier, and supersession relation.\n\nUse calibrated labels. Distinguish proved, exact under assumptions, empirically supported, disfavored, falsified, open, and superseded. Never translate \u201cnot observed\u201d into \u201cruled out.\u201d\n\n### 7. Run a hostile audit\n\nAttack the strongest plausible version of each alternative, not a straw man. Check for:\n\n- hidden memory or inaccessible information;\n- tail escape and high-to-low feedback;\n- topology or observable mismatch;\n- nonnormal amplification and unstable error propagation;\n- unjustified interchange of limits;\n- easier-model substitution;\n- nuisance explanations matched only by weak controls;\n- numerical conditioning, discretization, leakage, or selection effects;\n- a result that validates one witness but not the intended existence claim.\n\nUse [adversarial-audit.md](references/adversarial-audit.md) and state what each surviving objection would change.\n\n### 8. Select one bottleneck\n\nRank candidate next steps by logical leverage, expected uncertainty reduction, mechanism relevance, and cost. Prefer the smallest step whose possible outcomes would change the research state.\n\nDo not optimize for volume of algebra, number of experiments, or apparent completeness. Avoid polishing downstream machinery while an upstream identification or compactness gap remains decisive.\n\nNormally pursue one bottleneck at a time. In an authorized multi-route proof search, retain a small structurally diverse portfolio and select one explicit bottleneck per active route using [proof-search-orchestration.md](references/proof-search-orchestration.md).\n\n### 9. Precommit any authorized experiment\n\nBefore implementation or execution, read [decisive-experiments.md](references/decisive-experiments.md) and freeze:\n\n- competing hypotheses;\n- the disputed mechanism and why the testbed preserves it;\n- primary metric and matched controls;\n- pass, fail, and inconclusive thresholds;\n- numerical validity gates;\n- replication rule and conditional branches;\n- exact budget and terminal stopping condition.\n\nUse the smallest nondegenerate testbed, not automatically the smallest instance. Do not launch exploratory grids when one theory-selected stress or ablation can discriminate the hypotheses.\n\n### 10. Update causally\n\nAfter a derivation or experiment, ask:\n\n1. What mechanism was supported, rejected, or left unresolved?\n2. Which exact ledger claims change status?\n3. Does a failure kill the broad conjecture, the current witness, one assumption, or only one proof route?\n4. Which earlier conclusion is superseded?\n5. Does the result authorize a precommitted branch? If not, stop.\n\nSalvage valid identities, tools, counterexamples, and mechanisms from failed programs. Do not preserve their invalid conclusion.\n\n### 11. Synthesize the current state\n\nLead with the strongest defensible conclusion. Report separately:\n\n- established results;\n- supported but unproved claims;\n- disfavored or falsified explanations;\n- unresolved decisive gaps;\n- superseded conclusions;\n- the single highest-leverage next action, if requested and authorized.\n\nWhen later work corrects earlier work, use the corrected result in the main narrative and record the supersession once. Do not accumulate mutually inconsistent report layers.\n\n## Decision rules\n\n- **Strong surrogate performance:** Treat it as evidence of utility, not hierarchy convergence or arbitrary-accuracy existence.\n- **A successful matched nonlinear or causal control:** Downgrade that particular mundane explanation; do not infer a theorem.\n- **A failed high-order trend:** Check parity, conditioning, common-reference comparability, and replication before inferring divergence.\n- **A correct local expansion with growing coefficients:** Preserve the local algebra while rejecting unsupported positive-horizon convergence.\n- **A stability theorem without a source estimate:** Record propagation control; leave error production open.\n- **Failure of one representation:** Distinguish failure of the explicit witness from failure of the broader admissible class.\n- **Confounded evidence:** Mark inconclusive and stop unless a resolution run was preauthorized.\n- **Budget exhausted:** Stop even when the scientific question remains open.\n\n## Output standards\n\nMatch the requested artifact. For a concise assessment, provide the current conclusion, decisive evidence, and leading gap. For a full research state, provide:\n\n1. canonical target and research contract;\n2. sharp conjecture;\n3. exact causal skeleton;\n4. claim and evidence ledger;\n5. adversarial alternatives;\n6. experimental evidence and validity limits;\n7. what is established, disfavored, falsified, open, and superseded;\n8. ranked proof obligations or next decision.\n\nKeep notation stable and minimal. Attach claims to evidence close to where they appear. State assumptions at the claim they support. Never hide a decisive gap in a generic limitations section.\n", "truncated": false}}

Use when the user asks to solve, prove, derive, analyze, verify, or correct a mathematical problem that is not primarily an incremental lesson about supplied technical documents. Infer the strongest reasonable interpretation without inventing assumptions, establish a minimal setup, use minimal notation, expose every nontrivial step, state and verify external theorem hypotheses, give proof architecture when useful, and silently audit the draft before answering.\n---\n\n# Solve Mathematics Rigorously\n\nProduce a complete mathematical answer rather than a staged document lesson, unless the user explicitly requests incremental pacing. Optimize simultaneously for correctness, rigor, clarity, and economy.\n\n## Determine the intended problem\n\nInterpret the strongest reasonable version supported by the wording and mathematical context. Do not exploit an omission to answer a trivial or uninteresting straw-man version.\n\nHandle ambiguity according to its effect:\n\n- If it does not change the substance, adopt the conventional interpretation and state it briefly.\n- If one reasonable assumption makes the intended problem precise, state that assumption before solving.\n- If several reasonable interpretations lead to materially different answers, ask for clarification or give a compact case split when that fully resolves the ambiguity.\n- If a missing assumption is necessary for the claimed conclusion, do not invent it silently.\n\nIf the stated claim is false, say so and give a decisive counterexample or contradiction. Present a corrected nearby statement only when it helps, and label it as a correction rather than the original result.\n\nAnswer the problem actually asked. Do not weaken a universal claim to a convenient special case, replace an exact question with a numerical approximation, or prove a converse instead of the stated direction.\n\n## Establish a minimal setup\n\nBegin with a compact setup or preliminaries passage when it reduces ambiguity or supports later reasoning. State only the necessary:\n\n- domain and assumptions;\n- definitions used in the solution;\n- boundary, regularity, integrability, independence, or nondegeneracy conditions that matter;\n- notation that must persist through the answer.\n\nReuse the problem's notation whenever possible. Introduce a new symbol only when the unabbreviated expression is genuinely cumbersome and recurs often. Define every new symbol before use and state its mathematical type when that is not immediately evident.\n\nDo not force a visible setup heading for a short elementary problem. Do not hide substantive assumptions in prose later in the solution.\n\n## Give the architecture before a substantial proof\n\nBefore a long or conceptually difficult proof, explain the plan in a short paragraph or compact list. Identify the main steps, cases, claims, or lemmas and how they combine to yield the conclusion.\n\nKeep the architecture at the level needed to orient the reader. Do not duplicate the detailed proof, and do not substitute a plan for the proof itself. For a routine calculation or one-step argument, omit the architecture.\n\n## Execute every nontrivial step\n\nDerive each nontrivial algebraic, analytic, probabilistic, geometric, combinatorial, or logical transition. Give enough detail for the user to verify the step without reconstructing missing reasoning.\n\nIn particular:\n\n- identify the definition, identity, inequality, or prior claim being used;\n- show substitutions, rearrangements, case transitions, and changes of variables that carry real mathematical content;\n- track domains, signs, quantifiers, equality conditions, exceptional cases, and possible zero denominators;\n- justify limit exchanges, differentiation or integration under a sign, convergence claims, and optimization steps;\n- distinguish exact arguments from heuristics, approximations, and numerical evidence;\n- show that intermediate claims actually imply the requested conclusion.\n\nAvoid words such as \u201cclearly,\u201d \u201cobviously,\u201d \u201cstandard,\u201d and \u201cit follows\u201d when they replace reasoning. They are acceptable only after the supporting reason has been supplied.\n\nDo not over-explain elementary arithmetic or definitions already established. Compress routine steps while preserving the chain of verification.\n\n## Invoke external results completely\n\nWhenever the solution relies on a nontrivial theorem or external fact:\n\n1. state the exact form needed, including its assumptions and conclusion;\n2. verify each assumption in the present problem;\n3. identify the current objects substituted into the theorem;\n4. state exactly what conclusion the theorem yields here.\n\nDo not merely name a theorem or call a fact standard. Prefer the relevant specialization over an unnecessarily general statement.\n\nVerify a recent, specialized, source-specific, or uncertain result against a primary or authoritative source before relying on it. Cite the source near the claim. Paraphrase the relevant result unless its exact wording matters; quote only a short compliant excerpt when useful.\n\nIf the required theorem is unavailable, uncertain, or does not apply, expose the gap instead of proceeding as though it were valid.\n\n## Use examples and checks proportionately\n\nInclude an example, boundary case, counterexample, or remark only when it clarifies an abstraction, tests a hypothesis, explains a consequence, or exposes why an assumption is needed.\n\nUse low-cost sanity checks when they add confidence, such as:\n\n- substituting the result back into the defining relation;\n- checking dimensions, signs, or units;\n- testing a boundary or small case;\n- comparing with a known special case;\n- deriving the same conclusion by a second short route.\n\nDo not add examples mechanically or let them replace the general argument.\n\n## Keep the presentation compact\n\nUse headings only when they make a substantial solution easier to navigate. Keep notation sparse and stable. Prefer explicit expressions to disposable shorthand.\n\nUse equations to carry mathematical content, not decoration. Avoid formula dumps, decorative summaries, and boxed equations. State the conclusion plainly.\n\nLet the problem's complexity determine the answer length. Preserve every necessary assumption, theorem check, and logical transition, but remove repetition and exposition that does not improve understanding or verification.\n\n## Audit and revise silently\n\nDraft the solution, then review it before sending. Check:\n\n1. Did the solution address the strongest reasonable interpretation rather than a straw man?\n2. Were all adopted assumptions stated, and were none invented merely to force the result?\n3. Was the setup sufficient but minimal?\n4. Was every persistent symbol necessary and defined?\n5. Did a substantial proof receive a useful architecture?\n6. Can the reader verify every nontrivial transition?\n7. Was every external theorem stated in the needed form, with its hypotheses checked and its conclusion applied explicitly?\n8. Were recent, specialized, or uncertain results verified from an authoritative source?\n9. Were edge cases, equality conditions, domains, and quantifiers handled?\n10. Does the final conclusion answer the exact question?\n11. Do examples or remarks earn their space?\n12. Is the answer as compact as rigor allows, with no boxed equations?\n\nRevise any failing part and repeat the audit until the answer satisfies the checklist as well as possible. Do not mention the drafting or audit process in the final answer.\n", "truncated": false}}

The contract prevents a formally true statement from evading the scientific question.\n\n## 1. Canonical target\n\nRecord the following before choosing a proof strategy or surrogate:\n\n| Field | Required specification |\n|---|---|\n| Object | Exact system, process, operator, distribution, or model |\n| Admissible class | Inputs, parameters, regularity, data, and initial conditions |\n| Dynamics or relation | Update rule, flow, equilibrium condition, or map |\n| Observables | State components or derived quantities to approximate |\n| Error | Norm, metric, probability mode, and reference object |\n| Horizon | Fixed time, compact interval, asymptotic regime, or all time |\n| Limits | Exact order of size, depth, step, sample, or continuum limits |\n| Approximation family | Permitted state, equations, basis, memory, and operations |\n| Provenance | Information from which coefficients and initial data may be computed |\n| Complexity | What may scale with accuracy, horizon, or regularity |\n| Invariance | What must not scale with ambient system size or a hidden oracle |\n| Restartability | Whether the approximation must continue from its current state |\n| Forbidden substitutions | Easier architectures, norms, limits, observables, or optimizers |\n| Authorization | Read-only, theory, design, compute, and budget constraints |\n\nIf a field is immaterial, say why rather than silently omitting it.\n\n## 2. Non-vacuity filters\n\nReject or explicitly exclude a candidate statement if it permits any of the following:\n\n1. **Trajectory playback:** Encode the answer as a time-indexed forcing, interpolation table, or oracle coefficient.\n2. **Hidden full state:** Call a representation \u201cfinite\u201d while one coordinate contains an unbounded function, history, matrix, dataset, or arbitrary-precision code for the original system.\n3. **Instance memorization:** Choose coefficients after observing the target trajectory unless system identification is explicitly the scientific problem.\n4. **Quantifier inversion:** Prove existence separately for each realized trajectory when a uniform construction over a problem class is required.\n5. **Ambient scaling:** Let state dimension or coefficient complexity grow with the system size that the approximation is meant to compress.\n6. **Blind metric:** Measure an error that can be small while the named observables or mechanism are wrong.\n7. **Easier-model substitution:** Change the architecture, dynamics, data geometry, optimizer, scaling, or limit order that defines the question.\n8. **Noncausal initialization:** Initialize from future observations or unavailable latent variables.\n9. **Nonrestartable closure:** Match a curve from time zero but fail to evolve from an intermediate state when autonomy is part of the claim.\n10. **Unbounded precision loophole:** Store arbitrary information in finitely many real numbers without a regularity, computability, or provenance restriction.\n\n## 3. Claim ladder\n\nWrite separate propositions when the following are logically different:\n\n| Rung | Claim |\n|---|---|\n| A | An exact identity or finite truncation is well defined |\n| B | The truncated or surrogate dynamics are internally well posed |\n| C | The surrogate matches tested instances |\n| D | Successive approximations converge in the stated topology |\n| E | The hierarchy limit equals the intended mathematical limit |\n| F | The approximation is valid on every compact horizon |\n| G | The approximation is uniform or stable for all time |\n| H | Some admissible finite witness exists |\n| W | The particular proposed witness succeeds |\n\nDo not use evidence for C to assert D, or failure of W to reject H.\n\n## 4. Sharp-conjecture template\n\nAdapt this template rather than copying it mechanically:\n\n> Let \\(\\mathcal C\\) be the stated admissible class and let \\(O[S](t)\\) be the named observable of the canonical system \\(S\\). For every accuracy \\(\\varepsilon>0\\), horizon \\(T<\\infty\\), and required regularity bound \\(R\\), there exists an admissible autonomous approximation \\(A_{\\varepsilon,T,R}\\) of complexity \\(N(\\varepsilon,T,R)\\), independent of the ambient scale and of the realized future trajectory, whose coefficients and initial state are computable from the permitted source data, such that\n> \\[\n> \\sup_{S\\in\\mathcal C_R} d_T\\!\\left(O[S],O[A_{\\varepsilon,T,R}[S]]\\right)\\le \\varepsilon.\n> \\]\n> The approximation remains restartable from its finite state and obeys the stated limit order and provenance restrictions.\n\nThen specify:\n\n- whether the bound is deterministic, in probability, almost sure, or in expectation;\n- whether \\(N\\) may depend on the data dimension, sample count, or other fixed structural quantities;\n- whether one construction works uniformly over \\(\\mathcal C_R\\);\n- what computable or regular coefficient class excludes real-number oracle encodings;\n- what observation map compares the approximation to the target.\n\n## 5. Falsifiers and witnesses\n\nName concrete outcomes with distinct logical force:\n\n- **Witness falsifier:** disproves the current representation or closure.\n- **Mechanism falsifier:** rejects a proposed reason for convergence or success.\n- **Uniformity falsifier:** exhibits a family on which required complexity or error escapes.\n- **Existence falsifier:** proves no admissible approximation in the full stated class can work.\n- **Proof-route falsifier:** invalidates one argument while leaving the conjecture intact.\n\nFor each, state the exact assumption or rung it affects.\n\n## 6. Contract-change rule\n\nWhen evidence suggests changing the problem:\n\n1. Preserve the original contract.\n2. State the proposed change and scientific reason.\n3. Explain whether it weakens, strengthens, or replaces the conjecture.\n4. Obtain user direction when the change is material.\n5. Never present results for the revised contract as answers to the original one.\n", "truncated": false}}

Between these times interpolate the\nraw parameters linearly and recompute all hidden preactivations and\nfeatures from the interpolated parameters. Finite gradient flow\nmeans the differential equations with the same right-hand sides\ndivided by \\(\\eta_n\\).\nAt GD mesh nodes, velocities mean right-hand derivatives; use the\nleft-hand derivative at \\(T_0\\) if it is a terminal mesh node.\n\nHere is one explicit, very conservative positive interval. Let\n\\[\n a=\\pi/2,\\quad A=a^2+e,\\quad\n M_p=2^{1/p}\\exp\\!\\big(A(2a+1)+2pA^2a^2\\big)\\quad(p=1,2),\n\\]\n\\[\n A_3=a^2+AM_1,\\quad\n C_3=(1+2a)\\exp((1+2a)A_3)+a^2,\\quad Q=a(1+C_3),\n\\]\n\\[\n C_2=(2Q+C_3)M_2+Q^2,\\qquad\n S_0=\\min\\{1,(2C_2)^{-1},(2C_3)^{-1}\\},\\qquad\n T_0=\\frac14\\min\\{S_0,(4a^2)^{-1}\\}.\n \\tag{4}\n\\]\nThe constants are deliberately not optimized. They are finite explicit\nnumbers determined by \\(\\arctan\\), not by an unproved continuation\nor moment assumption.\n\n## Conclusions on this interval\n\nThere are three fixed neuron probability spaces, with bounded initial\nmatrix actions \\(W^{(2)}_0,W^{(3)}_0\\) and their reverse actions.\nThey are constructed from the joint limits of finite Gaussian matrix\ncalculations, preserving every reused transpose. There is a unique\nlocal uncut population state\n\\[\n X^{(1)}(t),\\qquad W^{(2)}(t),\\qquad\n W^{(3)}(t),\\qquad W^{(4)}(t),\\qquad 0\\le t\\le T_0.\n \\tag{5}\n\\]\nHere \\(X^{(1)}=F(Z^{(1)})\\), \\(F(z)=z+z^3/3\\);\nthe two matrices are bounded actions between the adjacent population\nspaces; and the readout is the typical population coordinate.\nInitially \\(Z^{(1)}_0\\) is standard Gaussian and \\(W^{(4)}_0=0\\).\nThis is a finite list of evolving fields and operators, not a\nfinite-scalar description.\n\nUse (1) on these spaces, replacing finite transposes by their reverse\nactions, denoted by a star, and using ordinary pointwise products.\nThen\n\\[\n f=E_3[W^{(4)}H^{(3)}],\\quad r=f-1,\\quad L=r^2,\n\\]\n\\[\n \\frac{dX^{(1)}}{dt}=-2r\\,(W^{(2)})^*\\delta^{(2)},\\quad\n \\frac{dW^{(2)}}{dt}=-2r\\,\\delta^{(2)}\\otimes H^{(1)},\n\\]\n\\[\n \\frac{dW^{(3)}}{dt}=-2r\\,\\delta^{(3)}\\otimes H^{(2)},\\quad\n \\frac{dW^{(4)}}{dt}=-2r\\,H^{(3)}.                       \\tag{6}\n\\]\nThe rank-one action is explicitly\n\\((U\\otimes V)B=U E[VB]\\).\nUniqueness holds among continuous integral solutions on these fixed\nspaces with bounded operator and mean-square parameter norms.\nThe same uniqueness holds when restarting from a reached state on\nany remaining subinterval of the constructed interval.\n\nIn the raw coordinate \\(Z^{(1)}\\), (6) is the gradient flow of\n\\(L\\): use ordinary mean-square lengths for the vector fields and\nthe square-summed matrix length for trained matrix changes.\nThe present state alone determines its derivatives; the Gaussian\nresponse coefficients in the proof are not prescribed external\ninputs to its evolution.\n\nFor each fixed same-layer finite list of network measurements,\nthe joint empirical law of both finite GD and finite gradient flow\nconverges uniformly in \\(t\\in[0,T_0]\\), in probability, to the\ncorresponding population law. Convergence includes second moments.\nThe class includes finite compositions of the current states,\nglobally Lipschitz coordinate functions, bounded products, and\nboth orientations of both current matrices; finitely many specified\ntimes may be used jointly. It also includes the named backward\nfields in (1) and the hidden preactivation and feature velocities.\nNo coordinatewise pairing across distinct hidden layers is asserted.\n\nEquivalently, for any such finite same-layer coordinate list and any\nfixed continuous measurement \\(\\psi\\) of at most quadratic growth,\nits empirical average approaches its population expectation uniformly\nin time. Thus prediction, residual, and loss converge uniformly.\nThe four finite kernel blocks\n\\[\n \\frac{\\|\\delta^{(1)}\\|_2^2}{n},\\qquad\n \\frac{\\|\\delta^{(2)}\\|_2^2\\|h^{(1)}\\|_2^2}{n^2},\\qquad\n \\frac{\\|\\delta^{(3)}\\|_2^2\\|h^{(2)}\\|_2^2}{n^2},\\qquad\n \\frac{\\|h^{(3)}\\|_2^2}{n}\n \\tag{7}\n\\]\nconverge uniformly to the expectation formulas in\nLOCAL_GRADIENT_STRUCTURE.md.\nFor every hidden layer, the empirical law of its entire preactivation\npath converges, including second moments of the supremum norm, to\nthe law of its population path. Integrated squared velocities\nconverge to the corresponding population integrals.\n\nWith the same finite initialization, GD and finite gradient flow\nalso approach one another uniformly in the state distance consisting\nof the normalized Euclidean difference in \\(F(z^{(1)})\\), the two\nmatrix operator-norm differences, and the normalized Euclidean\nreadout difference. No operator-norm comparison across different\nwidths or between different underlying spaces is asserted.\n\nEvery hidden layer learns a nonconstant feature trajectory.\nMore precisely, there are nonzero square-integrable variables\n\\(V^{(\\ell)}\\) such that\n\\[\n Z^{(\\ell)}(t)-Z^{(\\ell)}_0=2t^2V^{(\\ell)}+o(t^2),\\qquad\n H^{(\\ell)}(t)-H^{(\\ell)}_0\n   =2t^2\\phi'(Z^{(\\ell)}_0)V^{(\\ell)}+o(t^2)\n \\tag{8}\n\\]\nin mean square, and both displayed leading variables have positive\nmean-square size. The three hidden kernel blocks are positive\nconstant multiples of \\(t^2+o(t^2)\\); hidden feature squared-speed\nintegrals have positive \\(T^3\\) leading coefficients. Explicit\ninitial Gaussian formulas are given in LOCAL_FEATURE_LEARNING.md.\nThe total kernel is nonconstant, the loss decreases, and the\nactivation's best affine-approximation error on each hidden\ndistribution remains strictly positive on a common initial interval.\n\n## How the premises are discharged\n\nThe proof is the following chain of proved lemmas, not a list of\nadditional assumptions on the original network.\n\n1. L3_FIXED_MESH_SOURCE_IDENTIFICATION.md proves the exact finite\n   Gaussian source representation, including both independent\n   matrices, both transposes, empirical training feedback, and\n   singular covariance cases.\n2. LOCAL_ACTION_SPACE_AND_FLOW.md realizes those finite calculations\n   on common population spaces and proves the fixed-clipping flow\n   and its fixed-clipping width limit.\n3. LOCAL_RESPONSE_BOOTSTRAP_AUDIT.md proves a tail bound uniform\n   in the Euler mesh and clipping level on the explicit \\(S_0\\).\n   Its displayed scalar equations are precisely the equations\n   proved in item 1 and realized by item 2.\n4. LOCAL_CUTOFF_BRIDGE.md uses this bound to remove clipping,\n   prove local uniqueness and restartability, and establish\n   convergence from the actual raw GD, not merely from a\n   transformed or population-feedback scheme. In particular its\n   stated representation premise is discharged by items 1--3.\n5. LOCAL_GRADIENT_STRUCTURE.md proves that the resulting autonomous\n   evolution is a genuine gradient flow with all four kernel\n   contributions.\n6. LOCAL_FEATURE_LEARNING.md derives the strictly nonzero initial\n   hidden movement from this flow and the initial Gaussian action\n   laws. Its local-flow premise is discharged by items 1--5.\n\nIndependent reviews are checking both the individual lemmas and\nthe compatibility of this chain. None of these statements proves\nthe all-finite-time continuation requested in the main goal.\n", "truncated": false}}

ASSISTANT: I’m keeping the exact learning-rate scaling, all three trained hidden layers, and convergence of both forward and backward matrix actions. Those define the problem. I’m leaving out coordinate changes, auxiliary approximations, previous results, and suspected bottlenecks so the statement does not suggest a proof route.

ASSISTANT: Yes—as a **complementary attempt**, not a replacement. A fresh thread may explore different approaches, although it may also rediscover old ones. I would give it only the statement below, withholding our notes until it has developed an independent approach. The statement specifies the mathematical destination without suggesting a proof strategy.

## Conjecture: autonomous three-hidden-layer mean-field gradient flow

Let \(\phi(z)=\arctan z\). Consider a network with one input and target, both equal to \(1\), and three hidden layers of width \(n\). Its parameters are
\[
z^{(1)}\in\mathbb R^n,\qquad
W^{(2)},W^{(3)}\in\mathbb R^{n\times n},
\qquad W^{(4)}\in\mathbb R^n.
\]
Here \(z^{(1)}\) is the first-layer weight vector evaluated at the input, and \(W^{(4)}\) is the rescaled output weight.

At step \(k\), define
\[
h_k^{(\ell)}=\phi(z_k^{(\ell)}),\qquad
z_k^{(2)}=W_k^{(2)}h_k^{(1)},\qquad
z_k^{(3)}=W_k^{(3)}h_k^{(2)},
\]
\[
f_{n,k}=\frac{(W_k^{(4)})^\top h_k^{(3)}}n,\qquad
r_{n,k}=f_{n,k}-1,\qquad L_{n,k}=r_{n,k}^2,
\]
and
\[
\begin{aligned}
\delta_k^{(3)}
&=W_k^{(4)}\odot\phi'(z_k^{(3)}),\\
\delta_k^{(2)}
&=\phi'(z_k^{(2)})\odot
       (W_k^{(3)})^\top\delta_k^{(3)},\\
\delta_k^{(1)}
&=\phi'(z_k^{(1)})\odot
       (W_k^{(2)})^\top\delta_k^{(2)}.
\end{aligned}
\]
The activation and its derivative act coordinatewise; \(\odot\) denotes coordinatewise multiplication.

Initialize all entries independently, across and within parameter blocks:
\[
z_{0,i}^{(1)}\sim N(0,1),\qquad
W_{0,ij}^{(2)},W_{0,ij}^{(3)}\sim N(0,n^{-1}),\qquad
W_{0,i}^{(4)}\sim N(0,n^{-2}).
\]
With \(\eta_n=n^{-2}\), train every block by the exact updates
\[
\begin{aligned}
z_{k+1}^{(1)}
&=z_k^{(1)}-2\eta_n r_{n,k}\delta_k^{(1)},\\
W_{k+1}^{(\ell)}
&=W_k^{(\ell)}
-\frac{2\eta_n r_{n,k}}n
 \delta_k^{(\ell)}(h_k^{(\ell-1)})^\top,
&&\ell=2,3,\\
W_{k+1}^{(4)}
&=W_k^{(4)}-2\eta_n r_{n,k}h_k^{(3)}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Use physical time \(t=k\eta_n\), interpolate the raw parameters linearly, and recompute the hidden quantities from them. The corresponding finite-width gradient flow replaces these updates by their differential equations. These dynamics use normalized Euclidean squared lengths for the vector blocks and ordinary Frobenius squared lengths for the matrix blocks.

**Conjecture.** The following unconditional statement holds.

There exist three fixed probability spaces \((\Omega_\ell,\mu_\ell)\), independent of width and time horizon, supporting a population state consisting of exactly
\[
Z^{(1)}(t),\qquad W^{(2)}(t),\qquad
W^{(3)}(t),\qquad W^{(4)}(t).
\]
Here \(Z^{(1)}\) and \(W^{(4)}\) are square-integrable scalar fields on \(\Omega_1\) and \(\Omega_3\), respectively, and
\[
W^{(\ell)}(t):L^2(\Omega_{\ell-1})\longrightarrow L^2(\Omega_\ell),
\qquad \ell=2,3,
\]
are bounded linear operators. Write \(\mathbb E_\ell\) for integration over \(\Omega_\ell\), and \((W^{(\ell)})^*\) for the adjoint, characterized by
\[
\mathbb E_\ell[U\,W^{(\ell)}V]
=
\mathbb E_{\ell-1}[V\,(W^{(\ell)})^*U].
\]

Define the population forward and backward quantities by the finite-network formulas above, replacing \(z,h\) by \(Z,H\), transposes by adjoints, and
\[
f(t)=\mathbb E_3[W^{(4)}(t)H^{(3)}(t)],
\qquad r(t)=f(t)-1,\qquad L(t)=r(t)^2.
\]
The evolution is
\[
\begin{aligned}
\dot Z^{(1)}&=-2r\,\delta^{(1)},\\
\dot W^{(\ell)}U
&=-2r\,\delta^{(\ell)}
       \mathbb E_{\ell-1}[H^{(\ell-1)}U],
&&\ell=2,3,\quad U\in L^2(\Omega_{\ell-1}),\\
\dot W^{(4)}&=-2r\,H^{(3)}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Initially \(Z_0^{(1)}\sim N(0,1)\) and \(W_0^{(4)}=0\). The joint initial operator actions must reproduce the Gaussian initialization through the empirical-convergence requirement below at \(t=0\), including arbitrary finite reuse of both matrices and their transposes. Each probability space is generated by these initial measurement fields; it may not contain additional information encoding the future training trajectory.

On these fixed spaces, the equations have a unique global continuous integral solution, with locally bounded field second moments and operator norms. The solution is uniquely restartable from every reached state. The four displayed objects constitute the entire evolving state: no external time-dependent coefficients or additional history are supplied.

Moreover, this is the gradient flow of \(L\), with squared length of a parameter variation given by
\[
\mathbb E_1[(dZ^{(1)})^2]
+\|dW^{(2)}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2
+\|dW^{(3)}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2
+\mathbb E_3[(dW^{(4)})^2].
\]
Here \(\|A\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2=\sum_j\mathbb E_\ell[(Ae_j)^2]\) for an orthonormal basis of the input space—the operator counterpart of squared Frobenius norm. The trained matrix increments, not necessarily the initial operators themselves, have finite such norm on every compact time interval.

The finite GD and finite gradient-flow systems converge jointly to this same population evolution as \(n\to\infty\), along the full sequence, in the following sense.

Take any fixed finite collection of same-layer vector measurements formed from the parameter fields and constants using:

- fixed globally Lipschitz coordinate functions and multiplication by bounded globally Lipschitz coordinate functions;
- normalized inner products;
- any finite, layer-compatible composition of the current matrices and their transposes.

The instructions are independent of \(n\). The collection may also include the named backward fields, hidden preactivation and activation velocities, and measurements at finitely many other fixed times. Population measurements use the same instructions, with expectations and adjoints replacing normalized inner products and transposes.

For every such collection \(v_n^1(t),\ldots,v_n^m(t)\), every continuous \(\psi:\mathbb R^m\to\mathbb R\) satisfying
\[
|\psi(a)|\le C\left(1+\sum_{j=1}^m a_j^2\right),
\]
and every fixed \(T<\infty\),
\[
\sup_{0\le t\le T}
\left|
\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n
\psi\!\left(v_{n,i}^1(t),\ldots,v_{n,i}^m(t)\right)
-
\mathbb E_\ell
\psi\!\left(V^1(t),\ldots,V^m(t)\right)
\right|
\xrightarrow{\mathbb P}0.
\]
This applies jointly to GD and finite gradient flow started from the same initialization, with corresponding limiting fields identified. GD velocities use right derivatives at mesh points and the left derivative at a terminal mesh point. No coordinatewise pairing between different neuron layers is asserted.

The empirical laws of entire hidden preactivation paths also converge: for every continuous function \(\Psi\) on \(C([0,T])\) of at most quadratic growth in the supremum norm,
\[
\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\Psi(z_{n,i}^{(\ell)}(\cdot))
\xrightarrow{\mathbb P}
\mathbb E_\ell[\Psi(Z^{(\ell)}(\cdot))].
\]
In particular, predictions, losses, integrated squared hidden velocities, and all four kernel contributions
\[
\left(
\frac{\|\delta^{(1)}\|_2^2}{n},\
\frac{\|\delta^{(2)}\|_2^2\|h^{(1)}\|_2^2}{n^2},\
\frac{\|\delta^{(3)}\|_2^2\|h^{(2)}\|_2^2}{n^2},\
\frac{\|h^{(3)}\|_2^2}{n}
\right)
\]
converge to their corresponding population expressions; the time-dependent quantities converge uniformly on \([0,T]\).

Finally, every hidden layer undergoes nontrivial feature learning:
\[
0<
\lim_{t\downarrow0}
\frac{\mathbb E_\ell[
|H^{(\ell)}(t)-H^{(\ell)}(0)|^2]}{t^4}
<\infty,
\qquad \ell=1,2,3.
\]

Prove or refute this conjecture as stated. A refutation must contradict a specified conclusion for the prescribed Gaussian-initialized system; failure of a particular proof strategy or a counterexample with different initialization does not suffice.
